Transfer Your Deezer Playlists Between Accounts

Have you created a new account, or another Family profile in Deezer? Do you want to transfer all the playlists from your old account to the new one? Don’t worry, it only takes a few simple steps! Jump to section: - How to transfer a playlist created by you to your other account - How to add any playlist to your account

How to transfer a playlist created by you to your other account

  1. From a computer, log in to your original Deezer profile
  2. Select Library(image: image (14).png)
  3. Select Playlists
  4. Select the playlist that you want to copy Warning: You will not be able to proceed if the playlist is set to Private. Select Edit (image: Edit.svg) to check your playlist’s privacy setting. Use the slider to change it
  5. Copy the URL address from the browser address bar
  6. Log out of this profile and log in to your new profile
  7. Paste the URL address you copied into the browser address bar
  8. Select the checkbox (image: Checkbox.svg) next to all the tracks that you want to transfer
  9. Select Add to playlist
  10. Select New playlist (image: AddToPlaylistCross.svg)

How to add any playlist to your account

You can add any playlist to your music library by simply adding it to your library. Note: This is not the same as transferring playlists. If you want to make changes to it, you will need to create it as a new playlist by following the steps above

Use Deezer’s Playlist Assistant

When making or editing music for playlist, you can use Deezer’s Playlist assistant to quickly add Suggested tracks, Favorite Tracks, Top Tracks, or songs from another playlist. Deezer’s playlist creator allows you to find all your favorite and recommended content in one place. Jump to section: - Requirements - How to create a playlist and add songs using Deezer’s playlist assistant - FAQ

Requirements

Playlist assistant is available for all users on mobile. The minimum version of Deezer that is required is 6.2.22 on Android or 8.34 on iOS. #### How to create a playlist and add songs using Deezer’s Playlist assistant 1. Go to Library(image: image (14).png) 2. Select Playlists 3. Choose an existing playlist or create a playlist 4. Select Add tracks 5. Use the sources displayed at the top of the screen to filter tracks from your personal recommendations, such as:

  • Suggested tracks
  • Favorite Tracks: the last 100 tracks you’ve added to your Library
  • Top Tracks: the 100 most listened tracks based on your recent listening history
  • Playlists: playlists you’ve created and recently updated or launched Note: Some sources may not be displayed, depending on your listening history. If you would like more sources to appear, try these tips
  • Select the Add tracks (image: AddTrackIcon.svg) icon A tick (image: Tick.svg) will be displayed to let you know the track has been added Note: Select the song to listen to a 30 second preview. The 30-second preview is not available for US-based users

FAQ

Simply select the tick to remove tracks from your playlist when you’re on the adding tracks page. To learn more about removing songs from the playlist itself, visit our Create A Playlist article Yes, you can access Deezer’s playlist maker when you create a new playlist or edit an existing playlist. The playlist assistant can be used on any playlist that you’ve created or on collaborative playlists. Deezer’s playlist creator uses sources, such as your Favorite Tracks, Top Tracks and Suggested tracks, from your recent listening history and recommendations. If you don’t have enough listening history or Favorite Tracks, the playlist assistant will not display all the music sources.

Customize Your Deezer App

Love the Deezer app but want to give it your personal touch? Interface customization is now available on your app, ready to light up your life! #### Who can use it? Deezer offers three different types of customization for your application. ##### App Icon Want a fresh new feel? You can now customize your Deezer app icon. We have 13 different layouts to choose from, and it is simple to change: 1. Go to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application 2. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of your application 3. Under Preferences, select Display 4. Under Appearance, select App icon 5. A new screen will appear, allowing you to select your choice of icon customization| 6. Once chosen, select Save

Note: You may need to restart the app before your customization changes take effect ##### App Theme You can also change the color theme of your app, bringing a new set of colors and themes to match your vibe: 1. Go to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application 2. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of your application 3. Under Preferences, select Display 4. Under Appearance, select App theme and color 5. A new screen will appear, allowing you to select your choice of color and theme 6. Select Color to change the color of your application (you have 10 options on light theme, and 10 options on dark theme) 7. Select Theme to switch between Default (your device default setting), Light or Dark theme 8. Once chosen, select Save

Customize your cover to your desired effect and select Stickers to add when ready 8. Once happy with your customization, select Save 10. You will now see your new cover image!

Deezer Audio Quality

Is your favorite guitar solo not as crisp as you’d like? Does the singer seem muffled? Are the synths sounding a bit odd? Try adjusting your Audio Quality. The Deezer app gives you four levels of audio quality to choose from, whilst Deezer on desktop gives you three levels of audio quality to choose from. These are measured in kilobits per second; the more kilobits you have, the better your sound quality. Jump to section: - Audio Quality - How to change your Audio Quality - FAQ

Note: The higher the bit rate, the more data will be used. If your mobile data package is limited, you may want to consider lowering the quality to preserve data. Higher quality streams may cause stuttering during playback if your connection speed is limited #### Audio Quality - Mobile Audio Quality: Basic: Standard: High quality: High Fidelity - Bit rate: 64kb/s: 128kb/s: 320kb/s: 1411kb/s - Format: mp3: mp3: mp3: FLAC

  • Desktop Audio Quality: Standard: Better: High Fidelity
  • Bit rate: 128kb/s: 320kb/s: 1411kb/s
  • Format: mp3: mp3: FLAC

How to change your Audio Quality

  1. Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application

  2. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of your application

  3. Select the Music section

  4. Select Choose audio quality

  5. Select a streaming option from Mobile data, WiFi or Google Cast Note: Each streaming option can be set to a different audio quality

  6. Select one of the three audio quality options: Standard (129 kbps), High Quality (HQ) (320 kbps), or High Fidelity (HiFi) (1411 kbps, FLAC).

  7. Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application

  8. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of your application

  9. Under Streaming quality, select a streaming option from Mobile data, WiFi or Google Cast Note: Each streaming option can be set to a different audio quality

  10. Select one of the three audio quality options: Standard (129 kbps), High Quality (HQ) (320 kbps), or High Fidelity (HiFi) (1411 kbps, FLAC).

  11. Within the audio player, select the audio (image: AudioDesktop.svg) icon

  12. Select one of the three audio quality options: Standard (129 kbps), High Quality (HQ) (320 kbps), or High Fidelity (HiFi) (1411 kbps, FLAC).

FAQ

No, unless you are using Deezer Free. Deezer Free users can only use Basic (64kb/s) or Standard (128kb/s) audio quality. Yes. You can choose the audio quality for your downloads by going to Settings → Offline & Data → Download quality, then selecting the quality you want: Standard, High Quality (HQ), or High Fidelity (HiFi). You can also enable Allow downloads using mobile data if you want to download music while using mobile data.

Deezer Content Limits

The amount of data you can add to your Deezer profile is infinite. However, after a certain amount of data has been added, you will not be able to see or access it. Remember that the content still exists, so shuffling will still play all of your content! Jump to section: - Content limits on Deezer

See the content limits for each area below. #### Content limits on Deezer - Android: iOS: Desktop - Favorite Tracks: 10,000: 10,000: 10,000 - Amount of Created Playlists: 2000: 2000: 2000 - Amount of Favorited Playlists: 4000: 4000: 4000 - Songs in Playlists: 5000: 5000: 5000 - Albums: 4000: 4000: 4000 - Artists: 4000: 4000: 4000 - Podcasts: 1000: 1000: 2000 - Mixes: 1000: 1000: 2000

Note: Artists and Mixes cannot be downloaded for offline listening Note: For offline listening, the content limits are the same, provided your device has enough storage space

Deezer Crossfade

A crossfade is when one track blends seamlessly into the next track in your queue, preventing a gap of silence between songs. On Deezer, you can set the crossfade to a time of your choosing. At the moment, crossfade is only available on the iOS app, but we’re working hard to make it available on all devices. This article will walk you through how to set it up on your Deezer account. Jump to section: - How to enable the crossfade feature

Note: Some android beta users can test the feature in the Deezer Labs #### How to enable the crossfade feature 1. Go to Settings (image: Settings.png) 2. Select Audio (image: Audio.svg) 3. Scroll down to the bottom and then adjust the slider as necessary

Note: To disable the crossfade, adjust the slider down to zero

Deezer Data And Smart Cache FAQs

Memory, storage and your Smart Cache settings may affect Deezer’s performance on mobile. You will find the most frequently asked questions below, helping you to adjust your Smart Cache settings and enhance the performance of the app. #### FAQ You’ll need at least 1GB of data to install the Deezer app. If your mobile is nearly full, it’s worth deleting some of your old data. The quality of music you stream affects storage, so if you select High Quality on your audio settings, your Deezer files will take up more space on your device. You can learn how to free up more space for Android devices here and iOS devices here. Deleting your Deezer downloads will also help to free up space. Note: If your device has an SD card, you can change the location of your storage to increase your device’s capacity If you find that your mobile device is sluggish, you can also check your memory usage and kill or disable any apps that are slowing down your device’s performance. Wallpapers and widgets can also affect performance. Depending on your device’s manufacturer, you can run an optimization tool for Android or iOS. Smart Cache is a temporary storage space for any tracks you’ve streamed from the Deezer app. This space allows Deezer to keep playing tracks even if you lose WiFi signal. Every time you play a song, it is downloaded to your device’s Smart Cache. Depending on your Smart Cache settings, the next track will also be downloaded to your device. When listening to a recently streamed track, if it is still in the Smart Cache, Deezer will play it directly without downloading it all over again to save data. Warning: Smart Cache is part of Deezer’s app space. By increasing or decreasing the Smart Cache size, you can adjust the amount of storage Deezer uses on your device. Note: Once full, your Smart Cache will delete older tracks. 1. Go to Settings (image: Settings.png) 2. Select Data and storage (image: DataAndStorage.svg) 3. Within Smart Cache, move the Space allocated for Smart Cache slider until you’re happy with the storage space that will be used

Gapless playback

(image: PR-EN-1280x840.png)

What is gapless playback?

Audio formats such as MP3 and FLAC add tiny gaps of silence before and after a track, which can alter the listening experience of live recordings and other content. Gapless playback removes these gaps, offering an uninterrupted playback listening experience for music lovers. #### Who can use it? For now, the gapless playback feature works on Deezer’s FLAC catalogue and therefore is only available to Deezer HiFi users on version Android 6.1.26 and iOS 8.12 or later. To enjoy this new uninterrupted playback you may need to update your audio settings accordingly and choose to stream FLAC content. Note: All users can now enjoy gapless playback! #### What content will be available in gapless playback? All our FLAC content is available in gapless playback. The majority of Deezer’s standard mp3 catalogue is now available to enjoy with gapless playback. #### Give your feedback Deezer values your feedback. Join the conversation at Deezer Community.

How to Link your Last.FM account to your Deezer account

Jump to section: - What is Last.fm, and how does scrobbling work? - Things to know before you start - How to link your last.fm account to Deezer - FAQ Linking and setup - Family and Duo plans - Scrobbling delays and missing tracks - Scrobbling quality and accuracy - Platforms and compatibility

If you love keeping track of your listening history, Last.fm is the perfect companion to Deezer. This article explains what Last.fm is, how scrobbling works, and how to connect your Last.fm account to Deezer so your listening activity is logged automatically. We’ve also included answers to the most common questions our support team receives about this feature. Note: Linking your Last.fm account is only possible via the Deezer web player. It is not available in the Deezer desktop app or mobile apps. #### What is Last.fm and how does scrobbling work? Last.fm is a music tracking service that builds a detailed record of your listening history over time. Every time you play a track, it is automatically logged on your Last.fm profile. This action is called scrobbling. A track is counted as a scrobble once you have listened to at least 50% of it, or for a minimum of 4 minutes (whichever comes first). If you skip a track before reaching that point, it will not be scrobbled. This is why your Last.fm count may sometimes differ from your Deezer play count, which registers a stream after just a few seconds. Scrobbles are then used to: Note: Due to a known behaviour with Deezer’s integration, tracks may sometimes be scrobbled even if you only listened briefly or skipped them early. This means your Last.fm history may occasionally include tracks you did not fully listen to. If accurate scrobble counts matter to you, look for other tools that might enforce the 50% rule before submitting a scrobble. - Build your personal listening profile and statistics - Generate personalized music recommendations - Show your most-played artists, albums, and tracks over time - Connect you with other listeners who share your taste

When your Last.fm account is linked to Deezer, your plays on Deezer are automatically scrobbled to your Last.fm profile in the background; no extra steps are needed each time you listen. Tip: If you use Last.fm mainly to track your listening stats, Deezer now has its own native stats features built in, including My Deezer Year (your annual listening recap) and My Deezer Month (a monthly summary sent by email and in-app). These work independently of Last.fm and don’t require any setup. #### Things to know before you start ##### Family and Duo plan members If you are a member of a Deezer Family or Duo plan (but not the plan admin), you may not see the Sharing Preferences section when you visit deezer.com/account/share. Don’t worry, there is a workaround available. Leave, link, and rejoin If the Sharing Preferences section is not visible: 1. Ask the Family or Duo plan admin to remove you from the plan 2. Log in to deezer.com/account/share and link your Last.fm account 3. Ask the admin to invite you back to the plan

Good news: once linked, scrobbling will continue to work even after you rejoin the Family or Duo plan. Several members of our community have confirmed this works successfully. Note: If you live in a country where Deezer Free is not available, leaving your plan temporarily may not be straightforward. In this case, we recommend contacting our support team for assistance. ##### Desktop app The Last.fm linking option is not available in the Deezer desktop app, and scrobbling from the desktop app is not fully supported, even when your account is linked via the web player. To link or manage your Last.fm connection, always use the web player at deezer.com/account/share. ##### Kid profiles Last.fm linking is not available for Kid profiles. Only standard Deezer accounts can connect to Last.fm. #### How to link your Last.fm account to Deezer You can connect your Last.fm account to Deezer through the Deezer web player. Once linked, your listening activity will be scrobbled automatically across all devices where you use Deezer (mobile, desktop, Android TV, and more). 1. Go to deezer.com/account/share on a web browser and log in to your Deezer account 2. In the Sharing Preferences section, find the Last.fm option 3. Select Link 4. You will be redirected to Last.fm to authorise the connection 5. Log in to your Last.fm account and grant Deezer permission to scrobble 6. Once authorised, you will be redirected back to Deezer — your accounts are now linked

Tip: You only need to link your account once. From that point on, all your Deezer listening activity will be scrobbled to Last.fm automatically, even on devices where you did not set up the connection. Note: Scrobbles may not appear on Last.fm instantly. There is usually a short delay of a few minutes. On some platforms, such as Android TV, scrobbles can take from 30 minutes to 2 hours to appear. This is normal behaviour. #### FAQ ##### Linking and setup The most common reason for this is that you are a non-admin member of a Deezer Family or Duo plan. The Sharing Preferences section is hidden for Family and Duo members by default. See the Things to know before you start section above for available workarounds. If you have a standard Deezer Premium or Free account and still cannot see the option, try logging out and back in, clearing your browser cache, and visiting deezer.com/account/share again. If the issue persists, please contact us with your user ID and the email of your Deezer account so we can check. Make sure you are visiting deezer.com/account/share using a web browser (not the desktop app or mobile app). The Sharing Preferences section is only accessible via the web player. Also check that you are logged in to the correct Deezer account. If you still cannot find the option, it is likely related to your plan type. See Things to know before you start for details. No. The Last.fm connection can only be set up via the Deezer web player at deezer.com/account/share. However, once linked, your Deezer plays from mobile and other devices will be scrobbled automatically. ##### Family and Duo plans The Sharing Preferences section is hidden for Family plan members, including those with independent accounts (with their own email and password). However, there is a workaround: if you temporarily leave the Family plan, link your Last.fm account, and then rejoin the plan, scrobbling should continue to work. See Things to know before you start for full details. Yes. As the plan admin, you should have full access to Sharing Preferences at deezer.com/account/share and can link your Last.fm account directly. If Deezer Free is not available in your country, leaving your Family plan to link to Last.fm may leave you without an active account until you are reinvited. In this case, we recommend contacting our Customer Support team, and we will do our best to help. ##### Scrobbling delays and missing tracks There is usually a short delay before scrobbles appear on Last.fm. On most platforms, this is just a few minutes. On Android TV, scrobbles can take between 30 minutes and 2 hours to appear. This is expected behaviour. If your scrobbles have not appeared after several hours, try unlinking and relinking your Last.fm account at deezer.com/account/share. If the problem continues, please contact us. Occasional longer delays, sometimes 24 to 48 hours or more, are a known pattern with Deezer’s scrobbling integration. These typically occur during periods of high load or brief technical issues between Deezer and Last.fm’s systems. When this happens, your scrobbles are queued and will eventually appear on your Last.fm profile, though the order may be random. If you experience a long delay, wait a few hours first. If scrobbles still haven’t appeared after 48 hours, switching temporarily to the Web Scrobbler browser extension can help ensure your listening history continues to be captured. Deezer and Last.fm track listening data independently and may not always match exactly. Deezer’s internal statistics (such as your monthly listening recap) are calculated based on streams logged within Deezer’s own system. Last.fm only counts tracks that were successfully scrobbled, which depends on the connection being active at the time of playback. Differences can occur if scrobbles were delayed or if the connection was temporarily interrupted. If you notice a large or consistent gap, please contact us with your user ID and the email of your Deezer account, and we’ll look into it. Don’t forget to share pieces of evidence so we can verify better the discrepancies. Duplicates happen when Deezer’s built-in scrobbling and a third-party tool (such as Web Scrobbler or Pano Scrobbler) are both active at the same time. To fix this, decide which method you prefer and disable the other. To disable the built-in Deezer scrobbling, go to deezer.com/account/share and unlink your Last.fm account. ##### Scrobbling quality and accuracy This is a known limitation of Deezer’s built-in scrobbling integration. The “&” (ampersand) character is not always transmitted correctly when your plays are sent to Last.fm, which can cause discrepancies in artist, album, and track names — for example, “Nick Cave & the Bad Seeds” may appear as “Nick Cave the Bad Seeds” on your Last.fm profile. Don’t worry, your listening history is still being recorded. If accurate metadata is important to you, there are two options: - Use a third-party scrobbling tool such as Web Scrobbler or Pano Scrobbler, which handle metadata more accurately - Become a Last.fm Pro subscriber, which gives you access to an auto-edit tool that can automatically correct known metadata issues for future scrobbles, as well as fix past ones in your library

Note: The “+” (plus sign) character causes an even more severe issue. Scrobbles for artists or tracks containing “+” in their name – such as “Florence + the Machine” or “Sigrid + Lauv” – may fail entirely and not appear on your Last.fm profile at all, rather than just being submitted with a corrupted name. The same workarounds above apply. This is a known behaviour of Deezer’s scrobbling integration. While Last.fm’s official standard only counts a track as a scrobble after you have listened to at least 50% of it (or 4 minutes, whichever comes first), Deezer’s built-in scrobbler does not always enforce this rule, meaning tracks can be submitted to Last.fm even when skipped early. If accurate scrobble counts matter to you, using a third-party tool such as Web Scrobbler or Pano Scrobbler will correctly enforce the 50% rule before submitting a scrobble. Related to the ampersand issue above, Deezer’s built-in scrobbling also does not always transmit additional information in brackets, such as “(Remastered)”, “(Deluxe Edition)”, or “(feat. Artist Name)”. This means tracks from deluxe editions may not be distinguished from standard versions on your Last.fm profile. The same workarounds apply: use a third-party scrobbling tool such as Web Scrobbler, or use Last.fm Pro’s auto-edit feature to correct these in your history. Yes, this is expected. Deezer’s built-in scrobbling integration does not support the “Scrobbling now” (now playing) feature on Last.fm. Your tracks will appear on your Last.fm profile only after they have finished playing or when the next track begins, not in real time. If real-time “now playing” display is important to you, the Web Scrobbler browser extension supports this when you listen via the Deezer web player, as does Pano Scrobbler on Android. Remember to disable Deezer’s built-in scrobbling first to avoid duplicates. ##### Platforms and compatibility Yes! Deezer scrobbles to Last.fm on Android TV. Please note that there is a delay of approximately 30 minutes to 2 hours before your scrobbles appear on your Last.fm profile. This is normal; there is no need to take any action. Yes. Once you have linked your Last.fm account via the web player, your mobile listening activity will be scrobbled automatically. You do not need to do anything in the mobile app itself. Scrobbling via the built-in Deezer connection does not reliably work on the desktop app and is not officially supported. If you want to scrobble from your desktop, maybe consider using other tools, like the Web Scrobbler browser extension with the Deezer web player. Yes. Once your Last.fm account is linked to your Deezer account via the web player, all your listening activity – on mobile, web, and Android TV – is automatically scrobbled to your Last.fm profile. Desktop app scrobbling is not reliably supported (see above). Remember: if you also use a third-party scrobbler on top of the built-in connection, you may get duplicate scrobbles. If you have a question that is not answered here, our Customer Support team is happy to help. Your feedback is valuable to Deezer. Join the conversation on Deezer Community. Want to learn more about the topics covered in this article? Check out the following articles: Deezer Family · Join and Use Deezer Family With an Independent Account · My Deezer Month: Your Monthly Music Journey

Volume Normalization on Deezer

Different songs and artists produce different levels of volume when listening to music. If you are someone who wants your volume to be consistent, Deezer includes volume normalization feature that adjusts sound to maintain the same volume when you are listening to different tracks. Jump to section: - How to use volume normalization - Audio quality

How to use volume normalization

  1. Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application
  2. Click on the Settings cog icon (image: Settings.png) at the top right hand side of your application
  3. Within the Preferences section, select Music
  4. Under the Audio preferences section, select Normalise volume - this is a slider which you can toggle on or off

Note: You also have the option to access the Audio settings from any track playing by selecting the (image: AudioOptionsInPlayerMobile.svg) icon located in the left corner of the player 1. Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application 2. Click on the Settings cog icon (image: Settings.png) at the top right hand side of your application 3. Select Audio (image: Audio.svg) 4. Under Playback, select Normalize audio

Note: You also have the option to access the Audio settings from any track playing by selecting the (image: AudioOptionsInPlayerMobile.svg) icon located in the left corner of the player 1. Select the Audio (image: AudioDesktop.svg) icon within the player 2. Select Normalize audio

Audio quality

Deezer Cloud-Based Queue List

We know how time consuming and important it is to set up a queue of your favorite music to listen to. There is nothing worse than losing it! That is why we ensure whatever queue you’ve set up on Deezer is stored in the cloud, making it easier to enjoy non-stop music across all your connected devices. You can switch between mobile, tablet or desktop without losing your queue list. Jump to section: - How does the queue list work? - Requirements - Other compatible devices - How to synchronize your queue - How to add a song to the queue - How to remove a song from the queue - How to arrange your queue

(image: Deezer - Queue List head image-1 (1).jpg) Note: The cloud-based queue list feature is not available to Deezer Free users #### How does the queue list work? The cloud-based queue list improves your cross-device listening experience by ensuring all of your content is stored in the cloud, rather than locally on just one device. All of your audio preferences, including repeat, shuffle and others, will also be synced. When you add a track to your queue, Deezer places it before the rest of your current playlist, album or mix continues. This means the track you add will play soon, instead of being added to the very end of the full list. For example, if you’re listening to an album and add a track to your queue, the added track will play before the album continues. After the queued track has played, Deezer will continue playing the rest of the album. #### Requirements You will require iOS version 7.7.0 or higher, or Android version 6.0.6.60. #### Other compatible devices - Apple CarPlay - Android Auto - Apple Watch - Wear OS by Google - Android TV

Note: Devices not listed may not be fully supported, but rest assured we’re working on rolling out this feature to all devices and third-party apps. #### How to synchronize your queue The synchronized queue-list feature now gives you more control over your playlists. Note: This feature is on by default and can only be changed on desktop. 1. Select the audio (image: AudioDesktop.svg) button from the player 2. Select Synchronized Queue-list (image: Slider.png)

Warning: Other content, such as podcasts and audiobooks, will not be synced when you switch devices. #### How to add a song to the queue To add a track to your queue list: 1. Select (image: ThreeDotsHorizontal.svg) next to any track 2. Select Add to queue. The selected song will be added to your queue and will play before the rest of your current playlist, album or mix continues. If you add several songs to the queue, they will play in the order you added them. Alternatively, select Play next. The selected song will be added to the top of your queue and will play next. If you select Play next for several songs, the last song you selected will play first. 3. You can also swipe right on a track to quickly add it to your queue.

  1. Select (image: ThreeDotsVertical.svg) next to any track

  2. Select Add to queue. The selected song will be added to your queue and will play before the rest of your current playlist, album or mix continues. If you add several songs to the queue, they will play in the order you added them. Alternatively, select Play next. The selected song will be added to the top of your queue and will play next. If you select Play next for several songs, the last song you selected will play first.

  3. You can also swipe right on a track to quickly add it to your queue.

  4. Select (image: ThreeDotsHorizontal.svg) next to any track

  5. Select Add to queue. The selected song will be added to your queue and will play before the rest of your current playlist, album or mix continues. If you add several songs to the queue, they will play in the order you added them. Alternatively, select Listen next. The selected song will be added to the top of your queue and will play next. If you select Listen next for several songs, the last song you selected will play first.

Tip: Within the player on desktop, select the queue (image: Queue.png) icon and then select Automated recommendations (image: Slider.png) to add music recommendations to the end of playlists and albums within the queue. Note: The swipe-to-add-to-queue option is not yet available for entire playlists or albums. #### How to remove a song from the queue To remove a song from your queue: 1. Within the player, select the queue (image: Queue.png) icon 2. Swipe the song you want to delete all the way to the left

  1. Within the player, select the queue (image: Queue.png) icon

  2. Swipe the song you want to delete all the way to the left

  3. Within the player, select Queue

  4. Hover over the song you want to delete

  5. Select the delete (image: DeleteDesktop.png) icon to remove the track

How to arrange your queue

To arrange the order of your queue: 1. Within the player, select the queue (image: Queue.png) icon 2. On the song you want to move, hold the hamburger (image: MoveAndDrag.svg) icon and drag it to your desired position

  1. Within the player, select the queue (image: Queue.png) icon

  2. On the song you want to move, hold the hamburger (image: MoveAndDrag.svg) icon and drag it to your desired position

  3. Within the player, select Queue

  4. On the song you want to move, click and hold down, then drag it to your desired position

Deezer Sleep Timer

We’ve all fallen asleep listening to our favorite tracks, or some peaceful lo-fi, and then woken up to hear a heavy bass line in our ears the moment our eyes open. Wouldn’t it be great if you could set a time for your music or podcasts to stop playing! Well, that’s exactly what we’ve created - Deezer’s sleep timer allows you to set a timer so that you can relax knowing your music or podcasts won’t be playing all night when you fall asleep. Jump to section: - Requirements - How to access the sleep timer - FAQ

Requirements

Deezer’s sleep timer tool is available worldwide and is compatible with minimum versions 6.2.16 on Android and 8.28 on iOS. #### How to access the sleep timer The sleep timer can be located by clicking on the the 3 dots in the top right corner of the song (image: WhatsApp Image 2025-04-09 at 11.24.52_636debeb.jpg) Select the Sleep timer at the bottom of the list (image: WhatsApp Image 2025-04-09 at 11.24.51_a29c9d6a.jpg) Select your desired duration Note: To turn off Deezer’s sleep timer feature, select the sleep timer and then select Turn off timer. To adjust the duration, select the Sleep timer and then select a new duration Deezer values your feedback. Join the conversation at Deezer Community. Want more insight to the topics explored in this article? Find out more at Deezer Sleep Timer #### FAQ Yes, the sleep timer is available on any content you listen to on the Deezer player.

How to download the app

Visit the App Store (image: iOSAppStore.svg) to download Deezer on iOS devices. The minimum version of iOS that you can use Deezer on is iOS 18.5 or above. If your device cannot run this or any later versions, Deezer will not be available for download. Visit the Google Play Store (image: GooglePlay.svg) to download Deezer on Android devices. The minimum version of Android you can use Deezer on is Android 8.0 or above. If your device cannot run this or any later versions, Deezer will not be available for download. The minimum version of Windows you can use Deezer on is 10 (64-bit) or 11 or above. If your device cannot run this or any later versions, Deezer will not be available for download. The minimum version of Mac you can use Deezer on is iOS 12 or above. If your device cannot run this or any later versions, Deezer will not be available for download. To download the Deezer desktop app for Windows or Mac, follow these steps: 1. Go to www.deezer.com/download. Tip: If the download doesn’t start straight away, click the download button again to retry. If the download still doesn’t begin, consider downloading from a different browser operator (e.g. Firefox) 2. Open your Downloads folder and double-click the app to start the installation process 3. Follow the steps to install the app 4. Once installation is complete, launch the app, login and enjoy

Tip: You can even listen via your browser using our web player. Our supported browsers are: Google Chrome, Firefox, Microsoft Edge, and Safari #### More ways to listen For devices such as sound systems, smart speakers, TVs, smartwatches, cars and games consoles, you will require the Deezer app specific to that device. From the obscure to the cult classic, Deezer’s search tool will help you explore millions of songs, playlists and podcasts. Knowing how to search for your desired tracks, including how to search by lyrics and tags, means you’ll never be without that song you’ve been meaning to listen to! See our top search tips and find out how to clear your search history on mobile and desktop devices. FAQ

Some artists, albums and songs are not currently available or may only be available in some countries or regions and not in others. This is because all content that appears on Deezer is licensed. If something doesn’t appear, it may be because the agreements with artists, labels or right holders are still underway. Ownership rights can also change hands, and, in the case of very old or obscure music, we may have been unable to identify the current rights holder. Our team is constantly in talks with artists, labels and rights holders to ensure you have access to the largest possible music catalogue. If you can’t find an artist or song you are looking for at the moment, check back over time and it may appear. Many artists make mixtapes freely available online today. For us to share them with you on Deezer, we still need to license them. Artists provide their music to us through an aggregator. We are happy to provide mixtapes on Deezer, but if a mixtape contains material, such as music samples, audio clips, video clips, and so on, created by someone other than the artist themselves, it is up to the artist and their team to gain permission from all the individual rights holders to use the content. Note: The same licensing and ownership constraints can also apply to videos and podcasts

The Risks Of Downloading A Pirated Deezer App

A pirated app is a fake app mimicking Deezer’s service. Hackers tweak and reuse Deezer’s technology to unlock Deezer Premium features. Pirated apps rely on a specific version of the Deezer app, so once you’ve logged in you won’t get any new features or app updates, including any bugs we’ve fixed. Pirated apps are not exclusive to a particular operating system, and can be found when downloaded for both iOS and Android. Jump to section: - The risks of using a pirated app - How to support the artists you love - How to download Deezer safely - FAQ

The risks of using a pirated app

In a world driven by convenience, the temptation to install, pirate, or download mod APKs from unofficial sources is strong, especially for music streaming services like Deezer. However, using a pirated Deezer mod APK involves significant risks. This section highlights the security, performance, and device integrity issues that users may encounter with unauthorized installations. - Security - Using a pirated apk app can cause significant security risks. Hackers can steal your personal information, damage your device, attack your network or use malware, spyware or viruses to penetrate your information - Performance - Installing a modded app can result in more frequent crashes, freezes and loss of data. Users may also experience voice and data issues with unreliable connections, inaccurate location data and dropped calls - Battery - Hacked apps can cause battery drain, shortening the life of your Android, iPhone, iPad or tablet device - Warranty - Rooting/jailbreaking can cause your device to go straight out of warranty and, if anything happens, your manufacturer will not cover damages - Services - Certain apps may no longer work on your device. You may also notice that apps no longer receive notifications and experience issues synchronizing data - Updates/New Features - You may be unable to access or apply future software updates and new features

These risks are not operating system or model specific, and pirated apps can affect both iOS and Android. Once you have compromised your technology, it can be very hard to restore your device to its factory state. #### How to support the artists you love Pirated apps do not support artists, musicians, composers, songwriters and podcasters. Digital streaming services like Deezer represent more than half of the global recorded music revenue and are an integral part of music. As part of our mission to be one of the industry’s fairest digital streaming services, we’re championing a User-Centric Payment System (UCPS) that you can read more about here. By using Deezer, users are showing support for the artists, musicians, composers, songwriters and podcasters you love. Sign up to one of our plans to listen to all your favorite songs, playlists, podcasts and more. Using the real Deezer is a small price to pay to support the artists you love. #### How to download Deezer safely The best way to ensure you are downloading the official Deezer app is through our website, where you will find the official link to our latest version on either your iOS or Android app store. Doing this will eliminate any chance of security risk, battery drain, loss of device performance and will ensure you get every latest update as soon as they are released! With Deezer, you can enjoy premium quality audio, download your favorite songs and playlists for offline listening, and choose between our free and premium plans to suit your needs. #### FAQ If you have downloaded a pirated app, it is advisable to remove the application in full by deleting it from your device. For an extra layer of security, you can reset your device settings to factory status - before doing so, make sure you save and can restore a backup version of your device without the pirated app downloaded. Unfortunately once a device has been exposed to a pirated application, the associated risks to the device such as battery drain and security performance issues will likely render the device ineligible for warranty from most retailers. Some devices have inbuilt software that will flag up when you’ve downloaded something that can cause security or performance risk to your device. However, pirated applications are increasingly adept at behaving and looking like the real thing. The best way to ensure that you have not downloaded a pirated app, is to delete and uninstall the app itself, before going to the official Deezer website and downloading it directly from there. It’s unclear how a pirated app interacts with other applications on your device, however the risks of downloading a pirated app can cause security risks, performance issues and invalidated warranty on your device, which could make the use of other applications more challenging. To ensure that you have fully removed the pirated app from your device, the best step is to reset your device to its factory settings and restore a previous version of your device, making sure that the previous version does not include the pirated version. Downloading the Deezer app directly from our website is the most secure way of ensuring you are downloading the official Deezer application.

Upload MP3s To Your Deezer Account

We know you want all your music in one place, without having to change between apps to listen to songs. That’s why we made it possible to add your own MP3s to Deezer to complete your ultimate music collection and carry on listening! Jump to section: - How to upload MP3s to Deezer - How to access MP3s from a mobile device - Upload limits - Uploading MP3s troubleshooting

How to upload MP3s to Deezer

Note: MP3s cannot be shared between Deezer accounts Note: it’s not possible to upload MP3s from your mobile device to Deezer. You will need to use the web version of Deezer 1. Go to Library(image: image (14).png) 2. Select My MP3s Tip: Click the More tab if you cannot see My MP3s 3. Select SELECT MP3s TO UPLOAD 4. Select the MP3 files from your computer

Note: Occasionally, uploading MP3s in VBR (Variable Bit Rate) format can cause playback issues. We recommend that you upload files encoded in the CBR (Constant Bit Rate) MP3 format. #### How to access MP3s from a mobile device You can access your MP3s on mobile after uploading them from a computer. 1. Go to Library(image: image (14).png) 2. Select Playlists 3. Select My MP3s

Note: You can favorite your MP3s and add them to other playlists, just like the rest of your music on Deezer. Unfortunately, you cannot access MP3s on your mobile device if you’re using a Deezer Free account Note: You cannot download an MP3 file from your Deezer application, only upload #### Upload limits - Make sure your MP3s are under 200 MB in size - There is a limit of 2,000 MP3 tracks that can be uploaded to your Deezer account

Note: When using Deezer on other devices, such as smartwatches, game consoles and TVs, you may not be able to access your personal MP3s #### Uploading MP3s troubleshooting If you’re having trouble uploading MP3s to Deezer: - Check for browser updates - See our troubleshooting page for more assistance

Why Do I Need To Update My Deezer App?

Updating your Deezer app only takes a few minutes and is vital to ensuring you get the best experience using Deezer. You don’t want to miss out on any of our great features! Once you have updated your app, you will benefit from the most stable performance and latest updates. Check out our most frequently asked questions below: #### FAQ There are several ways to update your mobile app. You can select the message from within your app or you can update directly with your Google Play Store or App Store. Note: Depending on your operating system and your device, you might have to update the operating system of your device first To make the most of your Deezer app on your mobile, we may prompt you with a message or email asking you to update your app. We always encourage you to use the latest version to improve the overall security and performance of the Deezer app. You will still be able to use older versions of the Deezer app already installed on your device. However, they may have limitations such as reduced audio quality. Warning: Only the latest version of Deezer will be shown in your app store. You will not be able to find or rate older versions of the Deezer app, and as a consequence, if you uninstall an older version, you will not be able to restore it This is because your app is not up to date with our latest technology. Android users that have a Deezer app version below 6.1.19 and iOS users that have a Deezer app version below 7.14 will not be able to stream above Standard quality. Also bear in mind that your phone’s operating system must be at least 8 for Android and 18.5.0 for iOS. Note: Deezer Free users can only stream at Standard audio quality

AdBlock

AdBlock (image: AdBlock.png) is a plugin that helps users block unwanted ads. This plugin helps improve your website experience, improving your user experience. Jump to section: - Whitelisting - How to whitelist Deezer on Chrome - How to whitelist Deezer on Safari

Note: These instructions are specifically written for the AdBlock plugin but may also apply to other adblockers Tip: You can find more help and submit tickets to AdBlock here #### Whitelisting Whitelisting allows ads to be shown on the sites you trust. With AdBlock you have the following options: - Pause on this site - Pause on all sites - Block an ad on this page - Don’t run on this page - Don’t run on pages on this site

Deezer Free is powered by adverts. If you would like to help Deezer support the artists and content you love, we recommend whitelisting Deezer. #### How to whitelist Deezer on Chrome 1. On Deezer, select the AdBlock (image: AdBlock.png) icon in the extensions bar 2. Within Pause on this site:, select Once or Always The red AdBlock icon will turn green

Note: You can also access More pause options for further customization options #### How to whitelist Deezer on Safari 1. On Deezer, select the AdBlock (image: AdBlock.png) icon to the left of the address bar 2. Select Pause

Alternatively: 1. From Settings, select Filter Lists and Whitelisting 2. Select Whitelisting 3. Within the address bar, enter deezer.com You will see a green check mark when deezer.com is successfully whitelisted.

Note: To remove a website, click on the trash icon

Every time you see or hear an advert on Deezer, an artist is being supported. Through advertising, we’re able to support artists and keep Deezer free for listeners like yourself. It’s not possible to opt out of adverts if you’re currently using our free service. Deezer Account Being Used On Another Device

Occasionally, you may encounter an error message informing you that your account is being used on another device. This doesn’t happen too often, but when it does, it could be due to: - Your account’s music already playing on another device that belongs to you - Your account’s music already playing on another device that someone else is using

This article will guide you in identifying the error and how to resolve it. Jump to section: - How to view your connected devices - What to do if you don’t recognize a device

Note: Deezer Family users should check that all family members are using the correct profile to listen to music. If two people are listening to music on the same profile, it will result in an error message informing you that your account is being listened to on another device #### How to view your connected devices If you see an error message indicating that music is being played on another device, it means Deezer is being used on more than one device or web page at the same time. Please check that you’ve updated your devices with these steps: 1. Click on your profile icon at the top left hand side of the application 2. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of the application 3. From the section titled Preferences, select Offline & data 4. In the Download section, select Manage my devices 5. You will see a list of the connected devices associated with your Deezer account

  1. Click on your profile icon at the top left hand side of the application

  2. Select the Settings cog icon at the top right hand side of the application

  3. Select Devices (image: Devices.svg)

  4. You will see a list of the connected devices associated with your Deezer account

  5. Go to your profile (image: Profile.png)

  6. Select Account Settings

  7. Select the My connected devices tab

What to do if you don’t recognize a device

If you’ve visited your devices page and spotted a device that you don’t recognize, it’s possible you may have left your account logged in on somebody else’s device. See our article on how to protect your Deezer account. Deezer values your feedback. Join the conversation at Deezer Community. Want more insight to the topics explored in this article? Find out more at How to keep your Deezer account SAFE and SOUND

Problems Logging In To Deezer

There are a number of different reasons why you might be experiencing issues logging in. First and foremost, and especially if you have multiple accounts, ensure that you are entering the correct details for the email you are using to log in. Our FAQ section will address some common issues you may face logging into Deezer such as how to login to your account, how to connect your devices if they appear offline and what to do if you think someone is accessing your account. Tip: On the app, select the eye icon next to the password field to make sure you haven’t made a typo #### FAQ Whether you’ve forgotten your email, your password or both you can find your account and reset your password with these steps: 1. Go to our password reset form 2. Enter your email address. If you have multiple email accounts and are unsure which is the one linked to your Deezer account, enter all of your email addresses You will receive a password reset email from Deezer 3. Select the link within the email to reset your password

Note: When your password is successfully updated, you will see a message on the screen If you do not receive an email, please refresh your inbox and check your spam and junk folders. While it can take up to 5 minutes to receive the email, in some exceptional cases it can take up to 30 minutes. Note: Password reset links expire after an hour for security reasons If you’ve temporarily deactivated your Facebook account, you may experience some trouble logging in. You’ll need the Deezer password that was sent to you in the welcome message when you signed up. You can also reset your password through Facebook or Deezer. Tip: Learn more about Facebook If you see “Connection to Deezer.com failed” or “Deezer is offline” when using Deezer, check your connection. If you are using mobile data, try switching to WiFi. Mobile and tablet users should also check their device settings and ensure flight mode is off. If your account looks different (e.g. you can’t find your playlists, favorite tracks or podcasts), it’s possible you’ve logged in to a second account created with a different email address. If you have recently upgraded, you’ll need to check the payment has been completed and that your login details are correct. Don’t panic! It’s time to protect your account and reset your password. Deezer values your feedback. Join the conversation at Deezer Community. Want more insight to the topics explored in this article? Find out more at Login to Deezer

Problems With Playing Music On Deezer Web App

Everyone knows that technology doesn’t always work the way we want it to. Issues happen, but we want to make sure you aren’t without your favorite tracks, podcasts and radio for too long. This article will guide you through what to do when you’ve got a connection issue on the web, security settings and how to check for whether there is a global Deezer problem - and how to get in touch. Jump to section: - Connection issues - Security settings - Check for breaking news on our support website

Connection issues

If the Deezer web player says you have no internet connection, try these solutions below: - Restart your browser: Try quitting your browser application and restarting. Then, return to Deezer - Check for updates: Sometimes your browser may have missed an update. Try updating it to see if it makes a difference. Deezer is supported on the following browsers. Select a browser to see how to update it.

Note: Only the latest two versions of each browser are supported - Google Chrome - Microsoft Edge - Safari - Firefox

  • Clear your cache: Try clearing your browser cache.

Tip: If you’re not sure how, just search for ‘how to clear my cache’, or take a look at one of these links for your web browser: Google Chrome, Microsoft Edge, Firefox, Safari

Then log out and log back in again. - Reduce your open tabs: How many browser tabs do you have open? Depending on the power of your computer, sometimes lots of open tabs can affect Deezer’s performance.

Note: If you’re having difficulties with the Deezer desktop app and this article doesn’t solve your problem, the best place to get help is through the Deezer Community. You’ll find solutions to issues with playback problems, playlist recovery, specific technical issues and other topics. #### Security settings Is there a security setting/firewall in place that is not recognizing Deezer as safe? Check for any Flash blockers, ad blockers or security plugins that could be interfering with Deezer and make sure they recognize Deezer as a safe site. #### Check for breaking news on our support website Check our support feeds for service issues. If there is an ongoing issue with the service, you will find the latest information on our dedicated breaking news page! Tip: Check our support Twitter page @DeezerHelp Deezer values your feedback. Problems With Playing Music On Mobile

If you’re seeing messages like “Error: Failed to play” or “The requested content is not loading”, don’t worry this article has got you covered. These errors usually relate to connectivity or cache issues. Whether your tracks are greyed out, playback stops after one song, or the app goes offline, follow the steps below to get things back on track. #### Quick Fixes to Try First ##### Restart everything - Turn your phone off and on - Close and reopen the Deezer app - Log out and back in to your Deezer account

Check your internet connection
  • Switch between Wi-Fi and mobile data
  • Reset your Wi-Fi by turning it off, waiting two minutes, and turning it back on
Make sure Deezer is up to date
  • Update the Deezer app from your app store
  • Update your phone’s operating system (Android or iOS)

Clear Cache (for performance issues or full storage)

  • Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of your application

  • Select the Settings cog from the top right hand side of your application

  • Within the section Preferences, select Offline & data

  • Under Deezer data, navigate to Smart Cache and select Empty Smart Cache

  • Navigate to your profile icon at the top left hand side of the application

  • Select the Settings cog at the top right hand side of the application

  • Go to Data and storage

  • Under Data and storage, click Clear

⚠️ This will delete your downloads too. But don’t worry you’ll be able to download them again easily. #### Reinstall Deezer - Uninstall the Deezer app - Restart your device - Reinstall the app from your app store - Log back in

💡 Need step-by-step help?See our reinstall guide here #### SD Card Troubleshooting (Android only) If you’re using an SD card and facing playback issues: - Uninstall Deezer - Turn off your phone and remove the SD card - Restart your phone and reinstall the Deezer app - Open Settings > Data and storage > Change storage - Turn off the device and reinsert your SD card

One Song Plays Then Stops?

This could be due to: - Battery saver or data saver mode being active - Antivirus apps interfering with background playback

💡 Try disabling those settings and see if playback improves. #### Still not working? Check the Deezer Support homepage for service alerts Visit Deezer Community to ask questions or find answers Having trouble offline?See our Offline Listening guide

Reinstall Your Deezer App

Nobody likes technical issues when they are trying to listen to their music. But when it comes to technical difficulties the simplest answer is usually the easiest one. If you are finding yourself struggling to connect to the Deezer app, or are having problems streaming your favorite songs, reinstalling Deezer is a good way of restoring the app and getting you back to enjoying the music you love. Jump to section: - How to reinstall your app - Helpful links

Note: Any offline songs, albums or playlists that were previously downloaded will have to be re-downloaded Tip: Before reinstalling your app, please see our related articles: Can’t play Deezer music on mobile, Can’t play music on web browser and Deezer data & Smart Cache #### How to reinstall your app 1. On the home screen, tap and hold the Deezer icon until it starts shaking 2. Select Remove app 3. Open the App Store and search for and install the Deezer app

Note: If you are unable to uninstall the app, please get in touch with the store that you bought the phone from as we’re unable to uninstall apps for you 1. Find Deezer in your list of apps 2. Tap and hold Deezer to bring up its settings 3. Select Uninstall 4. Restart your phone 5. Open the Google Play Store and search for and install the Deezer app

Note: If you are unable to uninstall the app, please get in touch with the store that you bought the phone from as we’re unable to uninstall apps for you This section is split into Windows and Mac. You can jump to each section here: - Windows - Mac

Windows

Deezer desktop app is available to all users. You’ll need a minimum version Windows 7 or later to reinstall the app. 1. From the desktop, press the Windows button 2. Select Settings 3. Select Apps 4. Select Apps & features from the left pane 5. Select the Deezer app 6. Select Uninstall, and then select Uninstall again to confirm 7. Reinstall the Deezer desktop app

Mac

Deezer desktop app is available to all users. You’ll need a minimum version macOS 10.10 or later. 1. Open the Deezer app on your Mac 2. Open Finder, then select Applications in the sidebar

Tip: If it’s not listed in your sidebar, return to the top menu, and go to Go and then Applications 3. Find the Deezer app and then drag it to the Trash 4. Close Applications 5. Re-download from deezer.com and install

Report a problem with music content on Deezer

Did you know that every week, millions of tracks are uploaded on Deezer by millions of artists? While the vast majority of this content will end up on the correct artist page, occasionally, a song or album can become misplaced or uploaded into the catalog of another artist. This is something that we work tirelessly to try and prevent, but errors do happen. This is why we would like to ask you, the fans, to report instances of this if you find them. On both the web and Deezer app, you will now be able to Report a problem if you discover incorrect information on an album and soon, tracks too. Report a problem on albums If you have noticed an issue on an album page select the button at the top right corner of the application, then select the last option Report a problem. You will then be able to choose which problem you’d like to report: - This album wasn’t recorded by this artist - select this option if you think the album is listed on the wrong artist - There’s an audio problem - select this option if the audio file of one or more tracks of the album sounds corrupted - Undesirable content - select this option if you think one or more tracks of the album break our terms of use

Note: This “Report a problem” option does not cover missing lyrics. For missing lyrics, please refer to the following article: Play, Share And Translate Lyrics with Deezer Once reported, these issues will be directed towards our dedicated team here at Deezer who will resolve them as soon as possible. Thank you for your help!

Run A Traceroute

Occasionally, when you contact Customer Service with a connection problem, we may ask you to perform something called a traceroute to help us find out what’s wrong with your Deezer connection. A traceroute shows the steps that an internet connection, like the one between you and the Deezer service, takes to reach its destination. Jump to section: - How to run a traceroute on Windows - How to run a traceroute on Mac

Before you contact us directly about connection or latency issues, please take a look at our troubleshooting articles. Note: You’ll need to use a desktop computer to perform a traceroute. If your issues are with our mobile app, you’ll need to connect your desktop to the same network as your phone, either through tethering or Wi-Fi #### How to run a traceroute on Windows 1. From your computer’s desktop, in the search field, type cmd 2. Right-click on Command Prompt and select Run as Administrator The Command Prompt application will open 3. Enter tracert -h 20 www.deezer.com if your issues are on browser Alternatively, enter tracert -h 20 api.deezer.com if your issues are on our mobile app. You’ll need to be on the same network as your phone Note: For music streaming issues where playback is stuttering, pausing or stopping entirely, enter traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a cdnt-stream.dzcdn.net Note: For other content issues, such as when artwork for an album cover isn’t loading, enter tracert -h 20 cdn-files.deezer.com, tracert -h 20 e-cdn-files.deezer.com, tracert -h 20 cdns-files.dzcdn.net, or tracert -h 20 e-cdns-files.dzcdn.net 4. Select the Enter or Return key on your keyboard Allow a minute or so for the traceroute to finish. When a new line of text appears in the command prompt, the traceroute is done 5. When it’s complete, select all the text and copy it (press Ctrl + A, then Ctrl + C). You can then paste (Ctrl + V) this text into your reply to us

Warning: If you receive an error message, you did not run the command prompt in Administrator Mode. Return to step two and try again. #### How to run a traceroute on Mac 1. From your Mac desktop, go to Applications, then Utilities and open the Terminal 2. Enter traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a www.deezer.com if your issues are on browser Alternatively, enter traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a api.deezer.com if your issues are on our mobile app. You’ll need to be on the same network as your phone Note: For music streaming issues where playback is stuttering, pausing or stopping entirely, enter traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a cdnt-stream.dzcdn.net Note: For other content issues, such as when artwork for an album cover isn’t loading, enter traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a cdn-files.deezer.com, traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a e-cdn-files.deezer.com, traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a cdn-files.deezer.net, or traceroute -m 20 -w 1 -a e-cdn-files.deezer.net 3. Select the Enter or Return key on your keyboard Allow a minute or so for the traceroute to finish. When a new line of text appears in the command prompt, the traceroute is done 4. When it’s complete, select all the text and copy it (press Cmd + A, then Cmd + C). You can then paste (Cmd + V) this text into your reply to us

Suspicious Activity On Your Deezer Account Help

You may at some point receive an email informing you that there has been suspicious activity on your Deezer account. These emails are sent when our monitoring system and automatic checks detect one of the following scenarios: - Scenario: Description - New sign-in: A new device or location that isn’t recognized by Deezer has been used to try and sign in - Suspicious activity: Your credentials may have been compromised - A suspicious login: There has been an attempt to log in with the same email address - To secure your account: Your credentials are the same as the credentials leaked from another site

Jump to section: - Signs of suspicious activity - How to protect your account - How your Deezer account may have been compromised - Need more help?

Signs of suspicious activity

  • Playlists are appearing that have not been created by you
  • Tracks are appearing in your Library that have not been favorited by you
  • There are unusual music recommendations in your Flow
  • You are unable to log into your account due to an unauthorized password change

How to protect your account

If you receive an email from Deezer informing you of suspicious activity, you will need to select the Reset your password button on the email. Alternatively, you can reset your password here. Tip: You’ll need a strong password that contains at least 8 characters. It should be unique and contain a combination of numbers, uppercase and lowercase letters, and symbols. Avoid using the same email and password combinations across multiple apps and sites Warning : If you do not receive an email to reset your password, it’s possible that your email has been changed. If this happens, additional assistance from our team may be required. #### How your Deezer account may have been compromised Your personal information could have been illegally acquired online, or you may have accidentally leaked your credentials on the web. The most common reasons for suspicious activity are: - A malicious virus or keylogging software installed on your computer or device - A phishing email sent by a malicious user - Using the same email and password combination across multiple accounts - Having a commonly used password. See the list here

Verifying a Suspicious Email

If you have received an email claiming to be from Deezer that offers prizes, tickets, promotions, or other unexpected rewards, and you are unsure whether it is legitimate, please do not click on any links or provide any personal information. To help us verify the email, please download the original message and save it in .EML format. Then send the file to our Support team for review. The .EML file contains important technical information that allows us to investigate the origin of the message. Once we receive the file, our team will review the email and confirm whether it was sent by Deezer or if it may be a phishing attempt. #### Need more help? If you continue to notice suspicious activity after resetting your password, you will need further help from our support team to secure your account. Deezer values your feedback. Join the conversation at Deezer Community. Want more insight to the topics explored in this article? Find out more at How to keep your Deezer account SAFE and SOUND

Where Has My Music Gone on Deezer?

Nobody likes technical issues when you are trying to listen to your favorite music. Unfortunately, technical difficulties do happen and there are a number of reasons why your music may not be appearing on your Deezer app. This article will guide you through how to troubleshoot if the music you have saved has gone missing. Tip: When you save music on Deezer, it is stored in your Library You can create multiple Deezer accounts, each with its own subscription, saved music, and playlists. If you are using multiple devices, please make sure you have logged in to the correct account. If you can’t log in to your account for whatever reason, please see our Problems logging in page. If you’re not sure whether you have a Deezer account, you can check if you have an account. If you’re logged in to the correct Deezer account, here are some possible reasons why you can’t find your saved content: - You’re not looking in the right place. Songs you favorite are stored in your Favorite tracks, albums you favorite are saved in Albums, playlists you favorite are saved in Playlists, etc. - There is a limit to the amount of tracks that are displayed in your Library. On mobile, you are limited to viewing 10,000 tracks from your library, but you can see the full list on desktop. Read more here Tip: Like your library list, playlists can only display a certain number of songs depending on your device - You’re trying to favorite content without an internet connection. Only some devices will allow you to save content offline. You can learn more here

If content is greyed out and won’t play, it may be because you’re not connected. See our Can’t play music page for more details. It may also be greyed out because it is no longer available. This can happen if licensing agreements with the artist, label or right holders have changed. Also note that playlists created by Deezer, other users and content creators may have had their content removed from public view. If you’ve downloaded music to your device and can no longer play it, it is because your subscription has changed or expired. You have to be on a Deezer Premium, Deezer Family, Deezer Duo or Deezer Student plan to download music. If you decide to unsubscribe from Deezer, your downloaded tracks will not be lost, but you will not be able to access them until you resubscribe. As long as you resubscribe with the same account, all your downloads will be there.

Miscellaneous

Artist-Centric Payment Model (ACPS)

In this article, we want to introduce one of the less understood areas of music streaming - how royalty payments work. We’ll explain a bit about how payments work under the system that all streaming players have today and how we could make it fairer for all artists and content creators by switching to a “Artist Centric” payment model. We want to encourage Deezer users to help us raise awareness of this issue with our newly launched #MakeStreamingFair initiative, as well as a website dedicated to a User-Centric Payment System (ACPS). You can find the website here. (image: image-1 (1).jpg) #### How do streaming royalties work? Today the music streaming industry distributes royalties based on a market share model. To put it simply - all streams on a streaming platform are added up, and artists get paid in proportion to how much their streams represent out of this combined total. This means popular artists and genres benefit over smaller local and niche acts. Smaller artists and more niche or local genres receive less than their fair share of the money, which, in turn, means that a lot of fans don’t directly support the diverse artists and genres they are actually streaming. #### How to use Deezer’s ACPS website To use our ACPS simulator you’ll need to be a paid subscriber. Before you start, make sure you log in to your Deezer Premium, Deezer Family or Deezer HiFi account on deezer.com. Once you’ve signed into you account, you can now access our simulator, where you’ll be able to see your top five artists and exactly how much of your Deezer paid subscription today goes to artists you actually stream. The simulator also shows what would happen if a user centric payment system were in place in the future. You can compare the results and share on social media. Note: If you are a Deezer Free user or don’t have an account, you can still visit our website to learn about the user-centric payment initiative, you just won’t be able to use the simulator. #### Benefits of a Artist Centric payment system Here are just a few of the advantages of switching to a user-centric payment model: - A fairer system for a wide range of diverse local and international artists and genres - Corrects distortions like age or listening habits (i.e. young users also tend to skip more, resulting in further distortion) - Helps tackle streaming fraud and bot activities

Get involved

We’re encouraging music fans like you to explore our new website. Help us spread awareness of a more fair way of streaming and ACPS on social media with our #MakeStreamingFair hashtag. Share the site directly with your friends. And show love for your favorite artists!

Deezer For Creators

Jump to section: - Availability - Features

Deezer For Creators helps you track the performance of all your content! You can access all the data associated with your streams using our analytical tools. With our web version you can manage your artist page, see your analytics, and check which playlists you’re featuring in. Managers, artists, labels, and distributors can all log in or claim access to a creator’s content. Tip: For more information about claiming access, visit here The mobile version is also available to download on the Google Play Store and the AppStore. You can share your latest releases on social media and check out key analytics and audience insights. Note: The mobile version is only available for artists and managers #### Availability Deezer For Creators is available worldwide for all content creators who have at least one track or episode live on Deezer. Note: Deezer For Creators is only available in English for now #### Features Deezer For Creators is designed to give you reliable visibility on both your content and audience. You will be able to understand your fans and how your latest releases are performing. With an account you can: - Go behind the scenes to analyze and understand your streams: Through Analytics, you get access to streaming figures, the amount of unique listeners, favorited content, and top playlists that you’re featured in. This will help to understand what marketing actions should be performed around a new release and identify the streaming activity of your content - Discover who is listening to help you connect with your audience: For each of your releases, you’ll get access to audience demographics, such as top countries, age, gender, subscription plan, and devices that are used to listen to you. This can help you to identify key cities for your tours, optimize your marketing campaign, and get closer to your listeners - Curate your artist profile to highlight what is important: In the artist edition section, you’ll be able to edit your artist page on Deezer. You can update your profile picture and add a bio and social media links. You can also highlight specific content, such as a playlist you’ve created or an album you’d like your listeners to know about
